## §3. THE LEMMA DIVERGENCE LIST — all 196 spans walked, with the reason for every decline

Verdicts: **=** agrees with the Clementine at the verse being glossed (or is a clean truncation of
it) · **≠** diverges, `[var:]` **fired** · **≈** diverges, `[var:]` **declined**, reason given ·
**tr** truncated address, nothing to collate · **gl** the glossator's own word, not scripture.

### 3.1 The 25 spans that are NOT plain agreements

| col | span | Clementine | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0268B | *perfecta dilectio foras mittat timorem* | 1Jo 4:18 *perfecta **caritas** foras **mittit** timorem* | **≈ declined.** *dilectio*/*caritas* is the standing Latin doublet for ἀγάπη and moves nothing; *mittat* is the subjunctive the gloss's own *ut*-clause requires (*ut … perfecta dilectio foras mittat*), i.e. syntactic assimilation by the citer, not a divergent copy-text. Logged, unmarked. |
| 0268C | *prope timentes eum salutare ipsius* | Ps 84:10 — identical | **=** (a mid-verse cut after *Verumtamen*) |
| 0268C | *Quidam enim ex his de longe venerunt* | Mc 8:3 *quidam enim ex **eis** de longe venerunt* | **≈ declined.** *his*/*eis* is a demonstrative-for-anaphoric swap with no sense change ("some of these"/"some of them"); the gloss turns on *de longe*, which is untouched. |
| 0268C | *Non inveni tantam fidem in Israel* | **Mt 8:10 verbatim**; Lc 7:9 reads *nec in Israel tantam fidem inveni* | **=**, and worth naming: this is the documented ✓-from-a-different-verse trap in its benign form. The gloss is expressly comparing Matthew with Luke here, so the Matthean form is the right text at this spot. |
| 0268D | *Et reversi sunt* | Lc 7:10 *Et reversi, qui missi fuerant, domum* | **≈ declined.** The address stops after two words and supplies *sunt* to close it; a lemma-address auxiliary, not a copy-text reading. |
| 0268D | *Sicut credidisti fiat tibi, sanatus sit puer* | Mt 8:13 *sicut credidisti, fiat tibi. **Et sanatus est puer in illa hora*** | **≠ `[var:]` FIRED.** Indicative → subjunctive changes a report into a wish, in a sentence whose whole claim is that Matthew *explains more plainly*. |
| 0269C | *Filius unicus matri suae* | Lc 7:12 *filius unicus **matris** suae* | **≈ declined as a `[var:]`, logged as an oddity (§5.2).** Dative for genitive is one letter and could as easily be our file as his; a `[var:]` would assert it is his. Rendered as printed ("The only son **to** his mother"). |
| 0270A | *Tu es qui venturus es* | Lc 7:19/20 *Tu es qui venturus es* | **=**. Migne sets it with a full stop, not a question mark; kept (Pattern 8). |
| 0270A | *Et respondens dixit illis: Euntes renuntiate Joanni quae vidistis* | Lc 7:22 *…quae **audistis et** vidistis* | **≠ `[var:]` FIRED.** Two words dropped inside the clause; the `etc.` truncation falls after it, not at it. |
| 0270B | *Deus salvos faciendi, et Domini Domini exitus mortis* | Ps 67:21 *Deus **noster,** Deus salvos faciendi ; et Domini, Domini exitus mortis* | **≈ declined.** Head-of-verse abridgment only; every printed word agrees. |
| 0270D | *Mollibus vestimentis indutum* | Lc 7:25 *mollibus **vestibus** indutum* (Mt 11:8 *mollibus vestitum*) | **≈ declined.** *vestimentis*/*vestibus* is a synonym pair for the same garment, and *vestimentum* is the word the gloss itself then expounds (*Per vestem, corpus humanum*). No sense turns on it. |
| 0270D | *Plusquam prophetam* | Lc 7:26 *plus quam prophetam* | **≈ declined.** Word division / orthography, Pattern 9. |
| 0271B | *Non est regnum Dei esca et potus* | Rom 14:17 *Non est **enim** regnum Dei esca et potus* | **≈ declined.** Dropped connective at the head of a quotation begun mid-sentence; the corpus does this constantly. |
| 0272D | *Daemonia septem* | Lc 8:2 *de qua **septem daemonia** exierant* | **≈ declined.** Inversion inside a two-word address; addresses are not quotations. |
| 0272D | *Supra petram* (VERS. 6) | **Lc 8:6 reads *supra petrum*** in the Clementine; *supra petram* is the Clementine's own wording at Lc 8:13 | **≈ declined, and flagged for the record.** The divergence is real but it runs the wrong way to blame: the Clementine's *petrum* at 8:6 is itself the anomaly, and Migne's *petram* is the reading of 8:13 eleven lines later on the same page. Nothing is at stake in the sense (both are the rock the gloss expounds). |
| 0273C | *Quod autem secus viam* | Lc 8:12 ***Qui** autem secus viam* | **≈ declined.** *Quod*/*Qui* assimilates the address to the parallel *Quod autem in bonam terram* of 8:15; the gloss immediately continues *Secus viam **seminantur, qui**…*, i.e. it construes the plural anyway. A gender/number relative has no English exponent (Pattern 9). |
| 0274B | *Dominus illuminabit abscondita tenebrarum* | 1Cor 4:5 ***qui et** illuminabit abscondita tenebrarum* | **≈ declined.** The relative is resolved to its antecedent *Dominus* because the citer starts mid-verse and needs a subject. Standard citation practice, not a copy-text reading. |
| 0274C | *Et quicunque non* | Lc 8:18 *et **quicumque** non habet* | **tr / ≈.** Truncated address; *quicunque*/*quicumque* is orthography. |
| 0274D | *Porro homines illi mirati sunt, dicentes: Qualis est hic?* | Mt 8:27 *Porro homines mirati sunt, dicentes : Qualis est hic, quia venti et mare obediunt ei ?* | **≈ declined.** Migne inserts *illi*. It adds nothing the sentence does not already have (the gloss's point is precisely *homines*, and it argues that word: *merito et ipsi homines sunt appellati*). The `?` closes where Migne closes it. |
| 0275B | *Quia intraverunt daemonia multa* | Lc 8:30 *quia **intraverant** daemonia multa **in eum*** | **≈ declined.** Perfect for pluperfect, plus a truncation before *in eum*. Tense in an address is not a proof-text reading, and the gloss expounds *multa daemonia*, not the tense. |
| 0275B | *Multitudinis credentium erat cor unum et anima una* | Act 4:32 *Multitudinis **autem** credentium…* | **≈ declined.** Dropped *autem* at the head. |
| 0276A | *Cupio dissolvi, et esse cum Christo* | Phlp 1:23 ***desiderium habens** dissolvi, et esse cum Christo, multo **magis** melius* | **≈ declined.** *Cupio* for *desiderium habens* is the standard Old-Latin/patristic short form of this verse and says exactly what the participle says; *multo melius* for *multo magis melius* stands **outside** the italics in Migne, in the gloss's own voice, so it is not a lemma at all. |
| 0276A | *manere autem in carne necessarium propter vos* | Phlp 1:24 ***permanere** autem in carne, necessarium propter vos* | **≈ declined.** Loss of the prefix in a compound whose simplex means the same thing here, and one letter-group could as easily be our file as his; logged in §5.2 instead. |
| 0276B | *Nam et ego novi* | Lc 8:46 *nam ego novi* | **≈ declined.** An inserted *et*. |
| 0276B | *Patrem puellae* | Lc 8:51 *et patrem, **et matrem** puellae* | **tr.** Address truncated to the half the gloss expounds (*Pater puellae, coetus doctorum legis*). |
| 0277B | *Convocatis autem Jesus* | Lc 9:1 *Convocatis autem **duodecim Apostolis*** | **≈ declined.** The address keeps the opening ablative absolute and jumps to the subject, dropping the ablative's own noun; *Jesus* as an expressed subject here is a known Vulgate variant, and the gloss glosses neither word. Ungrammatical as printed, and rendered as printed. |
| 0277B | *ut nihil tollerent in via nisi virgam tantum* | Mc 6:8 ***ne quid** tollerent in via, nisi virgam tantum* | **≈ declined.** *ut nihil* for *ne quid* is the same negation by a different construction, and the gloss's entire argument (*ut nihil nisi virgam ferrent, et ut nec virgam ferrent*) rests on *nihil nisi virgam*, which is intact. **Negation preserved on both sides.** |
| 0278C | *Et discumbere omnes* | Lc 9:15 *et discumbere **fecerunt** omnes* | **≈ declined as a `[var:]`, logged as an oddity (§5.2).** The address as printed has no finite verb. A dropped *fecerunt* is exactly the shape a line-turn produces in our source, so I will not assert it is Migne's. |
| 0278C | *Omnis enim caro fenum* | Is 40:6 *Omnis caro **foenum*** | **≈ declined.** Inserted *enim* (the gloss is using it as a premise) and *fenum*/*foenum* orthography. |
| 0280B | *multi vocati, pauci electi* | Mt 20:16 *Multi **enim sunt** vocati, pauci **vero** electi* | **≈ declined.** A proverbially abridged tag; both particles are connectives. |
| 0280B | *Et factus est* (VERS. 29) | Lc 9:29 *Et **facta** est, dum oraret, species vultus ejus altera* | **≈ declined as a `[var:]`, logged as an oddity (§5.2).** *factus*/*facta* is one letter, and it is the letter that decides whether the subject is *species* or Christ. I rendered the masculine that is printed ("And he was made"), because the gloss immediately reads it of Christ (*Transfiguratus non substantiam verae carnis amisit*) — but a one-letter difference is suspicion of our file as much as of his, so no marker. |
| 0280C | *nondum apparuit quid erimus, sed tandem similes ei erimus, quando videbimus eum sicuti est* | 1Jo 3:2 *et nondum apparuit quid erimus. **Scimus quoniam cum apparuerit,** similes ei erimus : **quoniam** videbimus eum sicuti est* | **≠ `[var:]` FIRED.** A whole clause of the received text is gone and two connectives are replaced; this is a divergent quotation, not an abridgment. |
| 0280D | *unum iota aut unus apex non praeteribit a lege* | Mt 5:18 ***jota unum** aut unus apex non praeteribit a lege* | **≈ declined.** Word order in a two-word pair, plus *iota*/*jota* orthography. Negation intact. |
| 0282D | *Quicunque susceperit puerum* | Lc 9:48 *Quicumque susceperit puerum **istum in nomine meo*** | **tr.** Truncation + orthography. |
| 0283B | *Dicimus ut ignis descendat de coelo* | Lc 9:54 *dicimus ut ignis descendat de **caelo*** | **≈ declined.** *coelo*/*caelo*, the corpus's normal spelling. |
| 0283B | *Et consumat illos? Et conversus increpavit illos et dixit* | Lc 9:54–55 *…et consumat illos ? Et conversus increpavit illos, **dicens** :* | **≈ declined.** *et dixit* for *dicens* is a finite verb for a participle across a verse join where the address is already splicing two verses. Nothing in the gloss turns on it. |
| 0283D | *Obliviscere domum patris tui* | Ps 44:11 *et obliviscere **populum tuum, et** domum patris tui* | **≈ declined.** The citer takes the half he needs and marks the cut with `etc.` |
| 0283B | *Filius hominis non venit animas perdere, sed salvare* | Lc 9:56 — verbatim | **=.** Negation intact both sides. |

### 3.2 The remaining 158 spans — agreements and truncated addresses

Walked line by line against the Clementine at the verse actually being glossed, not merely against
the brief's ✓. They fall into three classes and **none** carries a divergence:

- **Verse-address lemmata, complete** (52): 0267D *Cum autem implesset* · 0268A *Intravit
  Capharnaum. Centurionis* · 0268A *Misit ad eum seniores* · 0268C *Gentem nostram* · 0268C *Jesus
  autem ibat* · 0268D *Non sum dignus* · 0268D *Nam et ego* · 0268D *Nec in Israel* · 0268D
  *Invenerunt servum* · 0268D *Et factum est* · 0268D *Quae vocatur Naim* · 0268D *Cum autem
  appropinquaret* · 0269C *Ecce defunctus efferebatur* · 0269C *Et turba* · 0269C *Et resedit* ·
  0269C *Et nuntiaverunt* · 0271B *Similes sunt pueris* · 0271B *Venit Filius hominis manducans et
  bibens* · 0271B *Justificata est sapientia* · 0271C *In domo* · 0271C *Coepit rigare* · 0271D *Is
  cui plus* · 0271D *Recte judicasti* · 0271D *Intravi in domum* · 0271D *Oleo caput meum non
  unxisti* · 0271D *Remittuntur ei peccata* · 0271D *Et coeperunt qui simul* · 0271D *Fides tua te
  salvam fecit* · 0272B *Et factum est* · 0272B *Maria quae vocatur Magdalene* · 0272D *Et Joanna* ·
  0272D *Procuratoris Herodis* · 0272D *De facultatibus* · 0272D *Cum autem turba* · 0272D *Exiit
  qui seminat* · 0272D *Secus viam* · 0273C *Haec parabola* · 0273C *Ad tempus credunt* · 0273C *In
  terram bonam* · 0273C *Nemo autem* · 0273C *Non enim* · 0274C *Qui enim habet* · 0274C *Et
  cessavit, et facta est tranquillitas* · 0274C *Mirati sunt ad invicem* · 0274C *Ad regionem
  Gerasenorum* · 0274C *In monumentis* · 0274C *Jesu Fili Dei* · 0274C *In principe daemoniorum
  ejicit daemonia* · 0274C *Vinciebatur catenis* · 0275C *Et rogabant* · 0275C *Grex porcorum* ·
  0275C *Fugerunt et nuntiaverunt in civitatem, et in villas* · 0275D *Exierunt autem videre quod
  factum est, et venerunt ad Jesum, et invenerunt hominem* · 0275D *Et rogaverunt* · 0275D *Redi in
  domum* · 0275D *In medicos* · 0276B *Tetigit fimbriam* · 0276B *Et confestim* · 0276B *Quis est* ·
  0276B *Videns autem mulier* · 0276B *Flebant autem* · 0276B *Nolite flere* · 0276B *Non est
  mortua* · 0276B *Tenens manum* · 0276B *Puella, surge* · 0276B *Quibus praecepit* · 0277A *Nihil
  tuleritis* · 0277C *Duas tunicas* · 0277C *Ibi manete* · 0277C *Pulverem pedum vestrorum* · 0277C
  *Joannes surrexit* · 0277C *Et assumptis* · 0277C *Secutae sunt illum* · 0277C *Dies autem
  coeperat* · 0277C *Nisi forte* · 0277C *Quinque millia* · 0277C *Distribuit discipulis suis* ·
  0277C *Et saturati sunt* · 0278D *Cophini duodecim* · 0278D *Et factum est* · 0279A *Quem me
  dicunt esse turbae?* · 0279A *Vos autem quem me esse dicitis?* · 0279A *Joannem Baptistam* ·
  0279A *Vos autem* · 0279A *Christum Dei* · 0279A *Praecepit ne* · 0279A *Dicebat autem* · 0279A
  *Abneget semetipsum* · 0279A *Nam qui* · 0279A *Hunc filius hominis* · 0279A *Aliqui hic stantes*
  · 0279A *Donec videant regnum* · 0279A *Post haec verba* · 0279A *Assumpsit Petrum* · 0280B *Et
  factus est* (see §3.1) · 0280C *Et vestitus ejus* · 0280C *Ecce duo viri* · 0280D *Erant autem
  Moyses, et Elias visi* · 0280D *Dicebant excessum* · 0280D *Gravati erant somno* · 0281A/0281B
  *Bonum est nos hic esse* (twice) · 0281B *Tria tabernacula* · 0281B *Unum Moysi* · 0281B *Facta
  est nubes, et obumbravit eos* · 0281C *Hic est Filius meus* · 0281C *Et timuerunt* · 0281C *Et
  vox* · 0281D *Inventus est Jesus solus* · 0281D *Factum est autem in sequenti die, descendentibus
  illis de monte* · 0281D *Occurrit [0282A] illis turba* · 0281D *Et ecce spiritus apprehendit* ·
  0281D *Et cum accederet* · 0281D *Et increpavit* · 0281D *Ponite vos* · 0281D *Futurum est ut
  tradatur* · 0282C *At illi ignorabant* · 0282C *Intravit autem cogitatio* · 0282C *Me recipit* ·
  0282C *Joannes dixit* · 0282C *Nolite prohibere. Qui enim* · 0283B *Domine, vis* · 0283B *Nescitis
  cujus spiritus estis* · 0283B *Dixit quidam* · 0283B *Vulpes foveas habent* · 0283B *Ait autem ad
  alterum* · 0283B *Sine ut mortui* · 0283B *Renuntiare his*.
  Each was read word by word against its own verse. Where a span stops short, it stops at a word
  boundary of the Clementine and nothing inside it differs.
- **Single-word addresses** (16): *fluctus* · *commotio* · *Visitavit* · *Beatus* · *Quid* ·
  *Justificata* · *Rogabat* · *Attulit* · *Maria* · *Centuplum* · *Videntes* · *Suffocantur* ·
  *Deridebant* · *Narraverunt* · *Sanabat* · *Qui autem* / *Dico autem* / *Qui non* / *Cui ergo* /
  *Arundinem vento* (short two-word addresses read the same way). Checked in place: each is the
  Clementine's own word at its verse. ⚑ **The two exceptions are `gl`, not scripture:** *fluctus*
  and *commotio* at 0268D are the glossator's **Hebrew etymology of Naim**, not a quotation, and
  must not be collated against anything. They are italicised because they are glossed words.
- **The `usque ad` splice formula** (10, at 0268D, 0269C ×2, 0270A, 0271C, 0283B ×5): Migne's own
  abridgment marker, not scripture. Rendered ***as far as*** in all ten, per the settled convention.

⚑ **One brief-file caution, recorded because it would mislead a later reader.** The brief prints
the `[0269C] *usque ad*` spans and the `[n:]`-bearing spans with a ✓, and both are meaningless
verdicts: *usque ad* occurs in the Clementine somewhere (in Genesis, in Kings), and a span whose
last token is a note marker was searched with the marker text stripped. Neither ✓ was clearance and
neither was treated as one.

---

## §4. CRUCES BY COLUMN — Migne's own, rendered as printed, no marker

**0269B — *Credente Domino sanatur servus, quia potest meritum Domini etiam servis suffragari.***
Migne capitalises *Domino*/*Domini*, and the easy reading makes this Christ. **It cannot be.** The
sentence's argument is that a *master's* merit avails for his *servants* ("not only by the merit of
faith, but also by zeal for discipline"), and the master in this pericope is the centurion, who is
the one who believed. Rendered "The **master** believing, the servant is healed … the merit of the
**master**." A mechanism-5 site, decided on the argument's own structure rather than on the capital
letter. **No emendation is involved**: *dominus* carries both senses and only English must choose.

**0270D — *Prophetico ergo exemplo ad virtutem subeundae virtutis hortatur.*** *virtutem …
virtutis* in four words, and the second occurrence has no work to do. Rendered as printed ("he
exhorts to the virtue of a virtue to be undergone"). I do not know whether this is a compositor's
repeat of a word from the line above, a scribal one in the recension, or the abridger's own
clumsiness, and I assert none of the three.

**0270D — *Ecce quae in veste pretiosa.*** Lc 7:25 reads *Ecce **qui** in veste pretiosa sunt*, and
Migne prints *quae*. ⚑ **This span is NOT italicised in the Latin**, so it is not in the brief's
inventory and no lemma test reaches it — but it is a quotation, and it is the only unitalicised one
in the range. A relative's gender has no English exponent (Pattern 9), so nothing changes in the
English; recorded so the divergence is not invisible.

**0270D — *exteros coelestis regni … consenescunt.*** *exteros* is accusative plural with no verb
to govern it; the clause's own verb is *consenescunt*, intransitive. Rendered as an apposition
("strangers to the heavenly kingdom, grow old…"), which is the closest the printed case allows.

**0271B — *Venit sicut tunc, ita et nunc, utramque viam salutis **respuitis**.*** A second-person
plural verb dropped into a third-person sentence, with no address. Kept ("you spurn both ways of
salvation"); the next sentence (*Lamentavimus … cantavimus*) is first-person plural, so the passage
is already changing person and the shift may be the abridger's compression of a homiletic original.

**0271C — *osculare, id est, nihil nisi sapientiam **loqueris**.*** Five imperatives (*recurre,
inquire, confitere, expande, osculare*) and then a future indicative. Rendered "thou shalt speak",
which is what is printed; *loquaris* would give the jussive the series wants, and I do not assert
it.

**0272B — *Sanata aegra, de salute ejus **aliis** aegrotant.*** *aliis* is dative/ablative and
cannot be the subject *aegrotant* requires. The obvious reading is *alii*, "others sicken over her
salvation" — which is exactly what the verse says (*coeperunt qui simul accumbebant dicere intra
se*) — and I declined to adopt it, because adopting it is the silent repair Pattern 7a exists to
forbid. Rendered "they sicken over her salvation, to others", which is deliberately awkward and
true to the page.

**0272D — *Joanna, Dominus gratia ejus, vel Dominus misericors**.** cujus est omne quod
vivimus.*** A full stop mid-sentence, followed by a lowercase *cujus*. Preserved exactly (Pattern
8, and 9001 @0202D is the precedent: the stop there was **our source's**, at Migne's line break).
⚑ **This is the site in my range I most want read at the plate**, because a stop that appears at a
line turn and a stop Migne set are indistinguishable in our file and only one of them is a defect.

**0273D — *vel per adversa terrendo, vel prospera blandiendo retardant.*** *retardant* is active
with no expressed subject: grammatically it must be the *adversa*/*prospera*, but the two gerunds
name the means, not the agent. Rendered with an unspecified "they hold them back". A mechanism-5
shape that has no safe resolution; recorded rather than resolved.

**0274A — *Quia voluptates et divitiae suis importunis cogitationibus guttur mentis, ne bonum
desiderium ad cor possit intrare quasi aditum vitalis strangulant flatus.*** The word order splits
*vitalis … flatus* across the verb and puts the purpose clause inside the object phrase. Every word
construes; only the order is violent. Rendered in the order English needs, with nothing added or
dropped, and logged because a later reader will meet the Latin and think something is missing.

**0274B — *Et bona terra cum patientia fructum reddit … et dum humiliter flagella **suspiciunt**,
post flagella in requiem **suscipiuntur**.*** Singular *terra* governs the first two verbs and
plural verbs govern the last two. Kept as printed; the sentence has slid from the ground to the
hearers it figures.

**0275A — *unde Gerasa sive Gergesi colonum ejiciens, id est diabolum a quo prius incolebatur vel
advena propinquans, qui longe erat in tempore.*** Two Hebrew etymologies run together with no main
verb: *Gerasa* = "casting out the settler", *Gergesa* = "a stranger drawing near". Rendered as
printed, with the two glosses left in apposition. *Gergesi* is a form I cannot vouch for (§5.2).

**0276A — *non capientes verbum Dei, et **in firma** mente pondus sapientiae non sustinentes*.**
Printed as two words. It construes as printed ("not sustaining the weight of wisdom in a firm
mind") and I rendered that, but *infirma* is equally available and would give "in an unsteady
mind", which suits *Conscii fragilitatis* better. **A word-division ambiguity is the shape our
source manufactures most often** (the hyphen-rejoin class), so this is a §5 entry and not a marker.

**0277D — *…Jesum resurrexisse negabant, multa mirabilia opera viderant, in cujus passione multa
miranda contigerant.*** The middle clause has no connective and no relative, and then *in cujus*
takes an antecedent the sentence never supplies in that position. Rendered as printed, asyndeton
and all.

**0278B — *Nondum intellexerant apostoli fidem populi credentis non esse venalem, sed **noverant**
nos potius esse redimendos per suas escas gratuitas.*** *Nondum intellexerant … sed noverant* is
self-contradictory as it stands. ⛔ **I did not supply a negative**, which is precisely the repair
7a forbids, and I did not soften *sed*. Rendered "had not yet understood … but they had known".
If a negative was lost here it was lost before our file, and the plate is the only thing that can
say so.

**0279A — *incomprehensibilia paternae **positionis** arcana*.** *positio* is a real word and gives
"the Father's position", which is not what the sentence wants; *dispositionis* would give "the
Father's disposing". A `[cj:]` was available and is declined per §1.1. Rendered as printed.

**0280A — *visuros **dilationem** Ecclesiae antequam per mortem recedant de mundo*.** Read as
*dilatio* = "delay", the sentence is nonsense; read as the attested medieval *dilatio* =
*dilatatio*, "spreading", it says exactly what the following clause says (*in praesenti contra mundi
gloriam erecti, certius credantur in coelo regnaturi*). I took the **attested medieval sense**
(Pattern 9: the test is attestation, not classicism) and rendered "the spreading of the Church".
**This is a rendering decision, not an emendation** — the printed letters are unchanged — but it is
a decision, and it is logged so it can be reversed.

**0280C — *ita et oculis mentis tuae jam **divinarum** sensus albescit **lectionem***.**
*divinarum* is genitive plural feminine and *lectionem* is accusative singular: **they cannot
construe together**, and the sentence needs *lectionum*. I rendered the genitive plural that the
adjective demands ("the sense of the divine readings"), and I say plainly that I am rendering
against one printed letter. **No marker**: one letter is suspicion of our file as much as of his.

**0281C — *cujus perfectus ostenditur audita voce dicentis*.** *perfectus* as a bare masculine
nominative has no referent; *perfectio* would give "whose perfection is shown". Rendered "whose
perfect one is shown", which is what the case allows.

**0283B — *presbyteros Baal morte affecit*.** 3 Kings 18 has Elijah killing the ***prophetas***
Baal, and *presbyteros* is a real word in the wrong place. Not a lemma, so no `[var:]` is even
available; rendered "the elders of Baal" and logged.

**0283D — *sed eum cujus patrem sciebat mortuum, illum patrem de quo dicitur: Obliviscere domum
patris tui, etc.*** The sentence never reaches a main verb: *sed eum … illum patrem …* dangles
after *elegit innocentem*. Rendered as printed, with the anacoluthon intact.

**0284A — *quid fiet illis qui nulla utilitate saepe **visitat** domos suorum quas dereliquerunt.***
Plural relative subject, singular verb, and a question that Migne closes with a full stop.
**Both kept**: "what will become of those who often, with no profit, **visits** the houses of their
own people which they had forsaken**.**" The period is Pattern 8, not 8b — 8b covers an *indirect*
question English cannot mark, and this is a direct one whose mark Migne simply does not print.

### 4a. MECHANISM-5 CENSUS — ALL SIX CHUNKS, not the first two

Every place in the range where a pronoun, relative or participle could bind to either of two nouns.
Decided on **case and gender**, then checked against the sense; the easier reading is named where
it differs. **Sites 12–19 are in chunks 0015 and 0016**, which is the half of a range that John's
census never reached.

| # | col | the ambiguity | bound to | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 0268B | *ad residentem in dextera Patris* — the one we come **to** | Christ, not the Father | *residentem* acc. sg. after *ad*; *Patris* is genitive |
| 2 | 0268C | *quae in centurione a Domino commendatur* | *fides* | fem. sg.; *quae* cannot reach *Israel* |
| 3 | 0269B | *Credente Domino … meritum Domini* | the **master**, not Christ | §4 above; the argument, not the capital |
| 4 | 0269C | *Quem sicut unicum deflet mater Ecclesia* | the dead man | masc. acc.; *Ecclesia* is the subject |
| 5 | 0270B | *Hoc non contra Joannem, quem mox adeo commendat* | John | masc. acc. sg. |
| 6 | 0270C | *in quem non est ita prodeundum* | ***mundus***, not *desertum* | *desertum* is **neuter**; *quem* is masculine. Grammar settles it and the easy reading ("into which desert") is the wrong one |
| 7 | 0271A | *in Ecclesia sanctorum **illi** praelatus sum* | John | the whole *Vel* is the speaker measuring himself against John |
| 8 | 0272C | *quibus postea sublimius volare … valeant* | the **feathers**, with the disciples as subject of *valeant* | *quibus* = *plumis*; *valeant* is plural with *discipulos* |
| 9 | 0272D | *quae quasi uxor erat prius diaboli* | the **Church of the Gentiles**, not Joanna | the whole participial string (*deditam … redemptam … impletam*) is accusative fem. sg. agreeing with *Ecclesiam*; *Joanna* is nominative |
| 10 | 0273B | *quo creaturae non erat accessus* | the Father's bosom | *sinu*, the nearest ablative, and the sense |
| 11 | 0275A | *quem daemonia negant aliquid secum commune habere* | **Christ**, not the *princeps daemoniorum* | *quem* is the acc. subject of *habere*; the sentence's whole force is that the demons disown him. The easy reading inverts the argument |
| 12 | 0276D | *Synagogam a Deo, **qui vita est**, vident alienam* | **God**, not the Synagogue | *qui* masc.; *Synagogam* fem. The contextually easier binding would make the Synagogue "life" in a sentence that is about her being cut off from it |
| 13 | 0277A | *qui si credere voluerint etiam subjecta Synagoga salva erit* | the Scribes and Pharisees | *qui* masc. pl.; *Synagoga* is the nominative of the apodosis |
| 14 | 0278B | *redimendos per **suas** escas gratuitas* | the Lord's foods | reflexive to the clause's logical agent, not to *apostoli*, who are the subject of *noverant* |
| 15 | 0279A | *a quibus suos distinguens ait* | **the crowds**; *suos* = his disciples | *quibus* pl. resuming *turbae*; *suos* is reflexive to Christ |
| 16 | 0280B | *qui fidem Trinitatis inviolatam servant* | those the three signify | masc. pl., generalising; not the three apostles alone |
| 17 | 0280C | *Quae vestis … sed Domino montem ascendente fulget* | the **garment** (the saints), not the Lord | *fulget* is singular with *vestis*; *Domino … ascendente* is an ablative absolute |
| 18 | 0281C | *ipsaque **qua** illustrabitur gratia, protegetur* | the **grace**, and the person protected is the believer | *qua* abl. fem. attracted into *gratia*; two different referents in five words |
| 19 | 0283A | *non sacramenta communia **in quibus** nobiscum sunt … sed divisionem … **qua** adversum nos sunt* | *sacramenta* / *divisionem* respectively | two relatives with two different antecedents inside one *non … sed*; ⛔ and *non* governs the whole pair, so **both** limbs of "detest and forbid" are negated in the English |
| 20 | 0283D | *sed eum cujus patrem sciebat mortuum, illum patrem* | the second disciple | §4 above; the sentence never completes |

### 4b. NEGATION — carried, including where it hurts

All of `non/nec/neque/nisi/haud/nunquam/nullus/nihil` in the range appear in the English and none
was added. The sites where carrying it produces awkward or contradictory English, and where I
carried it anyway: **0270C** (*nec … nec … nec … nec*, four limbs on John the reed, all four kept)
· **0271B** (*nec psalmis … nec threnis*, both kept, and the second *nec* governs a long
parenthesis that could easily swallow it) · **0273C** (*nulla fide, nullo intellectu, nulla saltem*
— three, kept) · **0275C** (*nisi quis more porci vixerit … non accipit … non autem ad perdendum*)
· **0277B** (*ut nihil nisi virgam ferrent, **et ut nec virgam ferrent*** — the gloss's point is
that the two commands contradict, so flattening either kills the paragraph) · **0278B** (*Nondum
intellexerant … sed noverant*, §4: the contradiction is on the page and stays there) · **0282A**
(*qui nec fidem confitentur, nec ipsius fidei audire volunt sermonem*) · **0283A** (*non sacramenta
… et adversum nos non sunt … et Dominum non sequuntur* — four negatives in one sentence with two
different scopes).

⚑ **7a⁗ is not at issue anywhere in this range**, because nothing is quarantined: zero `[sic:]`,
zero `[cj:]`, and the three `[var:]` sit **beside** a complete rendering and carry no force of their
own. Every verb, negation and conjunction is in the running English.

⚑ **And per the brief's closing warning, I re-read every ruling above against the English I actually
shipped.** The four that were most at risk of the John @0409B failure (a crux that says the right
thing over an English that does not) are 0269B *master*, 0272B *aliis*, 0278B *sed noverant*, and
0280A *dilationem*: all four read in the shipped chunks exactly as this file says they do.

## §5. THE DECLINED-ODDITY LIST — every suspect word I did NOT mark, in one place

⛔ **I do not know whose any of these is.** Each could be Migne's type or Corpus Corporum's
transcription of it, and my guess carries no evidential weight — the brief gives the numbers. They
are ordered by column so a plate reader can work down a page.

### 5.1 Non-words and broken words — the highest-value entries

| col | printed | what it looks like | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **0274C** | ***jutius*** — *ut de negligentia jutius puniatur* | *justius*, one letter (`s`) short | **The only outright non-word in 6,297 words.** I rendered "the more justly" and fired nothing. ⚑ It is **not** the hyphen-rejoin shape (it is not two halves of a real word), so the John mechanism does not explain it and a dropped sort is as likely as a dropped character. |
| **0276A** | ***in firma*** *mente* — *pondus sapientiae non sustinentes* | *infirma*, split across a word boundary | Pattern 10's split direction, which **is** greppable and **is** the class our source manufactures at line turns. It construes either way, which is why it is dangerous: I rendered "in a firm mind" and the opposite reading is at least as good. |
| **0272D** | ***misericors. cujus*** — full stop, then lowercase | a stop at a line turn | 9001 @0202D exactly: there the stop was **ours**, injected at Migne's hyphenation, and the English was about to publish "many. bands of the faithful". Same shape, same refusal to guess. |

### 5.2 Single wrong letters and wrong endings — real forms, wrong place

| col | printed | the form the sense wants | why I fired nothing |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0269C | *Filius unicus **matri** suae* | *matris* (Lc 7:12) | dative for genitive, one letter; a lemma, so a `[var:]` was available and declined per §3.1 |
| 0272B | *de salute ejus **aliis** aegrotant* | *alii* | one letter, and it is the letter that supplies the subject |
| 0278C | *Et discumbere omnes* | *et discumbere **fecerunt** omnes* (Lc 9:15) | a whole word absent from an address; a line-turn loss and a deliberate truncation look identical |
| 0279A | *paternae **positionis** arcana* | *dispositionis* | a lost prefix; the shape of a hyphen-rejoin, but *positio* is a real word |
| 0280B | *Et **factus** est* | *facta* (Lc 9:29, with *species*) | one letter, and it decides the subject; see §3.1 |
| 0280C | *jam **divinarum** sensus albescit **lectionem*** | *lectionum* | genitive plural demanded by *divinarum*; the two printed words will not construe |
| 0280A | *visuros **dilationem** Ecclesiae* | *dilatationem*, or the medieval *dilatio* = "spreading" | rendered on the attested-medieval reading, §4 |
| 0281C | *cujus **perfectus** ostenditur* | *perfectio* | a masculine nominative with no referent |
| 0284A | *illis qui … saepe **visitat** domos* | *visitant* | plural subject, singular verb; rendered as printed |
| 0276A | *manere autem in carne* | *permanere* (Phlp 1:24) | a lost prefix inside a lemma; `[var:]` declined per §3.1 |

### 5.3 Real words that merely read oddly, and proper names I cannot vouch for

| col | printed | note |
|---|---|---|
| 0270D | *ad virtutem subeundae **virtutis*** | the word repeats four words after itself and the second has no function; §4 |
| 0270D | *Ecce **quae** in veste pretiosa* | *qui* at Lc 7:25; **unitalicised**, so no lemma test reaches it |
| 0270D | *exteros* coelestis regni | accusative with nothing to govern it |
| 0271B | *utramque viam salutis **respuitis*** | a 2nd-plural verb in a 3rd-person sentence |
| 0271C | *osculare, id est, nihil nisi sapientiam **loqueris*** | future indicative closing a series of five imperatives |
| 0275A | *Gerasa sive **Gergesi*** | the expected form is *Gergesa*; a Hebrew place-name in an etymology is exactly where a compositor slips, and exactly where I have no control |
| 0275A | *vel **advena propinquans**, qui longe erat in tempore* | the second etymology, run into the first without a verb |
| 0278A | *postquam **perfecta** est mentio passionis Joannis* | *facta* would be idiomatic; *perfecta* is a real word and construes |
| 0283B | *presbyteros* Baal | *prophetas* at 3 Kings 18; not a lemma, so no `[var:]` was available |
| 0274A | *quasi aditum vitalis strangulant flatus* | word order splits *vitalis … flatus* across the verb; §4 |
| 0277D | *…negabant, multa mirabilia opera viderant, in cujus passione…* | asyndeton plus a relative with no antecedent in position |
| 0278B | *sed **noverant** nos potius esse redimendos* | the contradiction with *Nondum intellexerant*; **the one site here where a lost negative is a live hypothesis**, and the one I most want a plate for after 0272D |

### 5.4 What I checked and did NOT log

Reported so the list can be trusted as complete rather than merely long: I did not log
band-arithmetic (false positive 1 — my anchors run 0268A→0271D, 0272A→0272B, 0272C→0276B,
0276C→0277A, 0277B→0281A, 0281B→0284A, all continuous, and **no text breaks mid-word or
mid-construction across any of the 65 anchors**); I did not log the recurring `(cont.)` heads at
0012, 0014 and 0016 (false positive: our chunk division); I did not log Migne's verse addresses
where they sit a verse off the words they quote (**0269C `VERS. 13.-- *Et turba*`** quotes Lc 7:11
or 7:12, and **0272D `VERS. 6.-- *Supra petram*`** takes 8:13's wording under 8:6 — Migne's
numbering is his and an *Ibid.*-class reference is not ours to correct); and I found **no
`( sic )`, no bracketed `Forte`, no `<LACUNA>`, no asterisk-keyed note and no foot-of-page
apparatus** anywhere in the six chunks, which agrees with the brief's false-positive 7.

# Cruces — 9000 *Evangelium secundum Lucam*, stint 0017–0020 (PL 114, cols 0284A–0298B)

Chunks **0017, 0018, 0019, 0020** — capita X, XI (and cont.), XII. 5,748 Latin words.
Written against `data/briefs/9000-launch-brief.md`, `translation-style.md`, the 9741 anchor
pair, and §1 of `src/english/8999/cruces.md` and `src/english/9001/cruces.md`.

---

## (a) THE POLICY I APPLIED — stated before the findings, so it can be argued with

### a.1 Markers

**I fired no `[sic:]`.** Not one, in 5,748 words. Every suspect word in my range — non-words
included — is rendered as printed and logged unmarked in §(e) for the plate reader. This is
John's result carried forward deliberately, on the brief's numbers: on 8999 the 23 leaf-reads
spent on fired markers produced **no** corrections and the nineteen declined oddities produced
**all seventeen**. I have no evidence about the plate, and I say so at every site rather than
guessing a class.

**I fired no `[cj:]`.** Two sites tempted one (0287C *cadit*, 0295A *mansuros*); both are
argued and declined in §(d). Where a `[cj:]` would have been fired, the running English would
still have had to carry the force outside the bracket — the 8999 @0415A rule — and at both
sites the printed reading construes well enough that quarantining it would have been the
riskier act.

**I fired seven `[var:]`, under one stated test:**

> ⭐ **Fire `[var:]` only where the divergence from the Clementine is larger than a single
> word — a substituted or omitted *phrase*, or two texts joined. Log every single-word lemma
> divergence unmarked.**

That test is the brief's own asymmetry made operational: *"an OCR letter-swap manufactures
broken words and cannot invent a semantically coherent divergent clause."* A one-word
divergence is inside the class our transcription can manufacture; a missing prepositional
clause or a conflation of two books is not. Three candidates were **withdrawn** by this test
after being drafted — 0288A *habet* for *habebit*, 0293B *Qui* for *quia*, 0297A *Vestiebat*
for *vestiebatur* — and each now stands in §(e) instead. Every fired marker cites
`sources/vulgate/clementine-flat.txt` by book chapter:verse.

Final marker counts for the range: **`[var:]` 7 · `[sic:]` 0 · `[cj:]` 0 · `[d:]` 0 ·
`[ed:]` 0 · `[nt:]` 0.**

### a.2 The inherited axes (§1 of the brief), applied from the first line

| axis | what my range contains | what I did |
|---|---|---|
| gloss voice not archaised | *dicit/ait* throughout | **says / said**, never *saith*. **Zero `-eth` forms anywhere in my four chunks**, inside italics or out. |
| *turba* → crowd | **1** noun (0284B *Messis, turba credentium*) | "the **crowd** of believers" |
| *multitudo* → multitude | **1** (0296A *multitudine divitiarum*) | "a **multitude** of riches" — not "abundance", which would have collapsed it toward *abundantia* two lines earlier |
| *daemonium* → demon | 21 forms of *daemon-* | **demon / demons / demoniac** throughout |
| *diabolus* / *satanas* → devil | 17 *diabol-*, 5 *satan-* | **devil**; *Satanas/Satan* as **Satan** |
| *natio* → nation | **0 occurrences** | nothing flattened (all `natio` greps in my range are substrings of *damnationis*, *condemnationem*, *animadversione*) |
| *gentes* → Gentiles / nations | 4 plural noun sites, all typological | **Gentiles** at 0289A *conversione gentium*, 0289C *gentes sapientiam*, 0291A *transivit ad gentes*, 0291A *a cordibus gentium* — every one stands in an explicit *Judaei*/*gentes* opposition |
| singular *gens* | 2 (0286D *ejusdem gentis*, 0290A *illius gentis*) | **nation** ("of the same nation", "of that nation"), per Mark's third class |
| *gentilis* | 2 (0285B *pejores gentilibus*, 0289B *derisionem gentilium*) | **Gentiles** — see the note below |
| *ethnicus* | **0** | — |
| *mysterium* / *sacramentum* | 3 / 3 | **mystery** / **sacrament**, 1:1 |
| *usque ad* (italic splice formula) | **6** (0284A, 0286D, 0288B, 0292B, 0294D ×2) | **as far as**, all six |

⚑ **The one place I extended an axis rather than applied it, said plainly.** *gentilis* is a
fifth word on the *gentes* axis and the brief does not rule on it. I render it **Gentiles**,
because it is the adjective of *gentes* and both my sites sit in the same Judaei/gentes
opposition (0285B *sunt pejores gentilibus*, after *post descriptae legis contemptum*; 0289B
*propter derisionem gentilium*, of the pagan god-name). I did **not** reach for "heathen",
which §1.4 reserves for *ethnicus* — a word my range does not contain. If a later stint has
*ethnicus* and *gentilis* in one sentence, this needs re-deciding; I do not.

⚑ **Non-italic *usque ad* was left as ordinary prose**, per the 8999 merge note that only the
italic formula was normalised: 0285A *usque adeo … ut* → "to such a degree … that"; 0290C
*usque ad adventum Filii Dei* → "**until** the coming of the Son of God"; 0294B *usque ad
arcana … penetrare* → "as far as the hidden things". The one scripture lemma that opens with
it — 0293B *Usque ad sanguinem Zachariae*, Lc 11:51 — is rendered **"As far as the blood of
Zacharias"** and its gloss's *Cur usque ad hunc* follows suit, so lemma and exposition agree.

⚠ **The measuring-instrument trap fired here too, and I checked before reporting.** A `\bturba`
grep over my four chunks returns three hits and **only one is the noun**: 0296D *mentem
per**turbat*** is the verb *turbare*, and 0294B *tanto intonante **turbine*** is *turbo*, a
whirlwind — rendered "at so great a thundering whirlwind", not a crowd. That is the 8999
false signal reproduced exactly in this range.

### a.3 Register and structure

- **Pattern 17** applied as a number marker: *thou/thee/thy* wherever the Latin address is
  grammatically singular (0285B *Vae tibi*; 0286B *Cum audis … agnoscis*; 0288C *Excita ergo
  somnum tuum*; 0292A *ut aut peccare desistas*; 0293B *Miserere animae tuae*; 0296C *Quia
  longa tibi tempora … promittebas*), **you/your** for every plural. No archaism is used for
  reverence: the singulars addressed to a sinner and to the reader take the same *thou*.
- **Pattern 7 / 7a.** Every `non`, `nec`, `neque`, `nisi`, `ne`, `nullus`, `nihil`, `nemo`
  the Latin prints is in the English, including the triple stack at 0297D (*Non tantum non
  timeatis ne … desint*) and the four-fold at 0288D. Nothing is smoothed, nothing supplied.
- **Punctuation follows the plate** (Pattern 8): Migne's commas after *est* at 0284A and after
  *eorum* at 0293D are kept; nothing is merged or split.
- **Markers sacred.** Anchors 15 / 15 / 11 / 16, in order, verbatim. Notes 3 / 5 / 2 / 9,
  verbatim and untranslated — **no `[n:]` in my range is editorial prose**, so no Pattern-15
  `[nt:]` is owed. Frontmatter copied verbatim. `## ` heads 1:1, `(cont.)` untranslated.
  Question-mark parity 0/0, 1/1, 1/1, 2/2.
- **Italic spans are 1:1 with the Latin in 0019 and 0020.** 0017 and 0018 each carry **one**
  more than the Latin, both accounted for: the `[var:]` at 0285B quotes the Clementine in
  italics inside the marker (0017), and *Aegyto* is carried in italics under Pattern 7 (0018).
- **Em-dashes: zero in running English** across all four chunks. The two that remain are
  inside `[var:]` apparatus.
- ⚠ `(Ephes, IV)` at 0290D is **not** a `[n:]` marker but a parenthesis inside the italic run;
  reproduced verbatim, comma and all.

### a.4 Re-reading my own rulings against the English I wrote

Per §4 of the brief (the 8999 @0409B failure: a crux that records the right decision over an
English that carries the wrong one), I re-read every ruling below against the shipped text
before closing. Three checks worth naming: 0290C's *Cum … fortis … armatus … custodit … atrium
suum* renders each fragment in the order Migne splits it, so the reader can reassemble Lc
11:21; 0295C's *in Filio hominis* really does read "**in** the Son of man" and not "against";
0297A's *Vestiebat* really is active in the running text ("Was clothing as one"), matching the
decline recorded at §(e).

### The seven `[var:]`, gathered

| col | span | Clementine | why it was fired and not declined |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0285A–B | *cui multum donatum est, multum ab eo quaeritur, et potentes potenter tormenta patientur* | Lc 12:48 + Sap 6:7 | two books joined under one citation, plus *donatum/datum* and *quaeritur/quaeretur* |
| 0289C | *Judaei signa petunt, gentes sapientiam* | 1 Cor 1:22 | ***gentes*** stands where the Vulgate has ***Graeci***, and *quaerunt* is gone |
| 0290C–D | *captivam ducens captivitatem … quosdam quidem apostolos, alios evangelistas* | Eph 4:8, 4:11 | **the prophets are missing** from 4:11; participle for finite verb in 4:8 |
| 0292D | *Qui facit voluntatem Patris mei, hic frater meus et soror et mater est* | Mt 12:50 / Mc 3:35 | Matthew's *Patris mei* inside Mark's frame |
| 0296A | *Non in solo pane vivit homo, sed in verbo Dei* | Deut 8:3 | *in **omni** verbo **quod egreditur de ore** Dei* is absent |
| 0297B | *Exaudiet me cras justitia mea* | Gen 30:33 | *Respondebitque mihi* → *Exaudiet me* |
| 0297C | *omnia cooperantur in bonum bonis* | Rom 8:28 | ***bonis*** stands where the Vulgate has ***diligentibus Deum*** |

⚑ **The three I drafted and withdrew** (0288A *habet*, 0293B *Qui estis*, 0297A *Vestiebat*)
are in §(e), not here. Each is a single word. Under the policy in §(a.1) a single word is a
claim about our file, not about Migne's plate, and I decline to make either claim.

---

## (d) THIS RANGE'S CRUCES, BY COLUMN

### 0284A — *Sicut in apostolis est, forma episcoporum*
Migne's comma stands between *est* and *forma*, breaking a nominal predicate. Preserved
(Pattern 8); the English reads "there is the form of bishops" and the comma is not reproduced
where English cannot carry it without becoming unreadable. No emendation, no marker.

### 0285B — *pejores gentilibus, naturale solummodo jus dissipantibus*
The participle is dative plural agreeing with *gentilibus*, not with the cities: it is the
**Gentiles** who squander only the natural law, and that is the whole force of *pejores*.
Bound accordingly. (Mechanism 5 candidate; checked, not a defect.)

### 0285B — *cum illae civitates praedictae, Domino praesente credere noluerunt, Tyrus et Sidon olim fuerunt amicae*
The sentence has no main clause answering *cum*: Migne joins a *cum*-clause to a bare perfect.
Rendered as it stands ("for whereas those foresaid cities … would not believe, Tyre and Sidon
were once friends…"), with nothing supplied. **No marker** — the words are all real and the
looseness is the recension's, not a type defect.

### 0286A — *ut unde illi ceciderunt sublevati, isti ascendant humiliati*
⛔ **Mechanism 5, and the easy reading is the wrong one.** *illi* are the fallen angels and
*sublevati* attaches to *them* ("from whence **they**, being lifted up, fell"); *isti* are the
disciples and *humiliati* attaches to them. Reading either participle across to the other
subject inverts the whole antithesis. Bound by case and number, not by sense.

### 0286B — *Cum audis omnia, agnoscis omnipotentem, non decolorem, non degenerem Patris*
Second person **singular** (Pattern 17) → *thou hearest / thou acknowledgest*. Both negatives
carried. The genitive *Patris* governs *degenerem*, not *omnipotentem*: "not degenerate **from
the Father**".

### 0286D — *ejusdem gentis* · 0290A — *illius gentis*
Singular *gens* of a people → **nation**, per §1.4's third class. Neither is *gentes*, and
neither is rendered "Gentiles".

### 0287A — *descendit quidem lex per Moysen in mundum, et nullam sanitatem contulit hujusmodi*
*hujusmodi* is the wounded man of the parable, not the law and not the world. Rendered
"conferred no health upon **such a one**". Mechanism-5 site; checked.

### 0287C — ⭐ *Quanto humilius sedet, tanto amplius **cadit*** — a declined `[cj:]`
Printed: *Quanto humilius sedet, tanto amplius cadit. Aqua confluit ad convallem de tumoribus
collis.* The expected sense is that the lower Mary sits the more she **takes in**, which is
what *capit* would give, and *capit* → *cadit* is a one-letter difference.

**Declined, and here is the argument for declining.** *cadit* is a perfectly good verb and the
sentence **construes without emendation** if the subject is the water of the very next
sentence: "the more humbly she sits, the more abundantly **it falls**" — water running down
from the swellings of the hill into the valley. Pattern 18 exists for a real word whose
faithful English *misleads*; here the faithful English is the image the next sentence supplies.
Rendered *"The more humbly she sits, the more abundantly does it fall."* I record the
alternative so a reader is not left to invent it, and I fire nothing. **Worth a glance at the
plate at 0287C**, but it is a reading question, not a type question.

### 0287C — *haec convivium parat Domino, in convivio Domini illa jam jucundatur*
*haec* is Martha (named second, nearer) and *illa* is Mary. Rendered **"the latter … the
former"** rather than "this one … that one", which in English inverts as often as not. The
alternative binding would have Mary preparing the banquet, which the same sentence denies.

### 0288A — *Quis vestrum **habet** amicum*
See §(e). Present for the Vulgate's future *habebit*. Rendered as printed; no marker.

### 0288B — *Psal. XLIV* for Ps CXLIV
The note keyed to *Prope est Dominus … voluntatem timentium se faciet* reads **(Psal. XLIV)**;
the text is **Ps 144:18–19**, and Ps 44 contains none of it. `[n:]` contents are Migne's
citations and pass through **verbatim and untranslated** — the brief forbids correcting or
querying one, and §6.3 forbids proposing corrections to references. Logged here only, so the
indexer's operator knows the `refKey` and the `refDisplay` will not agree at this site.

### 0288C — *Excita ergo somnum tuum*
*excitare somnum* — literally "rouse thy sleep". Rendered **"Shake off thy sleep"**, which is
the idiom's force, not a repair of a defect: nothing here is defective. Recorded because the
literal English would be nonsense and I want the choice visible.

### 0289C — *et domus supra domum cadet cum habitatoribus unius domus ab alterius domus familia vel opprimuntur vel puniuntur*
Two things at once: a singular *cadet* followed by plural *opprimuntur*/*puniuntur*, and a
*cum*-clause with no conjunction to hang the plurals on. ⛔ **Mechanism 5 checked:** the
ablative agent *ab alterius domus familia* is the **other** house's household, and the ones
oppressed are *habitatoribus unius domus*. Getting that backwards is the easy reading and the
wrong one. Rendered as printed, number mismatch included; **no marker** — every word is real.

### 0290A — *qui sibi conscii erant nihil malarum artium a Domino didicisse*
*qui* is the apostles (the *filii Judaeorum* of the lemma), not the Jews who accuse them.
The English binds it to the apostles. Mechanism-5 site; checked.

### 0290B — the split lemma *Profecto pervenit … regnum … Dei: … Regnum*
Migne breaks Lc 11:20 into four italic fragments and glosses between them. All four
reproduced in place and in order, with the colon after *Dei* preserved.

### 0290B — *Hunc digitum magi confessi sunt in **Aegyto***
See §(e). Carried in italics, untranslated, unmarked.

### 0290C — the six-fragment lemma *Cum … fortis … armatus … custodit … atrium suum*
The gloss dismembers Lc 11:21 and glosses each word. All six fragments kept italic in the
printed order with the gloss words between them, so the verse reassembles on the page.

### 0292A — *quae in Ninivitis per poenitentiam … in regina austri studium percipiendae sapientiae … congregatur*
*quae* is the Church (from *Ecclesiae mysterium*), and *studium* is a bare nominative in
apposition with no verb of its own. Rendered as an apposition inside dashes-free parenthesis;
nothing supplied. Mechanism-5 site: *quae* could be pulled to *poenitentiam*, and is not.

### 0292B — *sed Judaei veram Dei sapientiam cum ipsis conversantem, non audiunt sed blasphemant*
⛔ *conversantem* is accusative and agrees with *sapientiam*: it is **Wisdom** that dwells
among them. Binding it to *Judaei* would make the Jews the ones dwelling, and lose the point.

### 0293C — *ne fidei vos studiosos faciat operum negligentes*
Both *studiosos* and *negligentes* agree with *vos*. Rendered "lest it make **you**, zealous
for faith, negligent of works."

### 0293B — *Qui estis ut monumenta* · *Repondens autem*
See §(e). Both rendered as printed; no markers.

### 0294B — *Hanc legisperiti, nec ipsi lege et prophetis intelligunt*
A bare ablative *lege et prophetis* where *in lege et prophetis* is expected. Rendered "neither
themselves understand **by** the law and the prophets". Real words; a possible lost *in*.
Logged in §(e).

### 0294B — *Intrare clavem, est non esse contentum superficie litterae*
*intrare* with an accusative object *clavem* ("to enter the key"). Real words, and the sense is
recoverable — to enter *by* the key. Rendered literally ("To enter the key is not to be content
with the surface of the letter"), **nothing supplied**. Logged in §(e).

### 0295A — ⭐ *vel mansuros in denuntiatione supplicii* — the second declined `[cj:]`
Printed: *Totus hic locus ad subeundam pro confessione Domini passionem vel contemptu mortis,
vel spe praemii, vel mansuros in denuntiatione supplicii instruit.* The first two limbs are
ablatives of means (*contemptu*, *spe*); the third is a **future participle in the accusative
plural** with nothing to agree with. It does not construe.

**Declined.** Every word is real and I can see at least two readings (*mansuros* as a second
object of *instruit* — "instructs also those who will abide"; or a corruption of an ablative
in the series). Choosing one in a `[cj:]` would be exactly the confident wrong call the brief
prices. Rendered as literally as the printed words allow — *"either by contempt of death, or by
hope of reward, or those who will abide, in the denunciation of punishment"* — which is
deliberately as awkward in English as it is in Latin. **Plate read owed at 0295A.**

### 0295C — *Et omnis qui dicit verbum **in Filio** hominis remittetur*
⛔ **The most consequential single word in my range.** Lc 12:10 reads *in **Filium** hominis* —
a word spoken *against* the Son of man. The ablative makes it a word spoken *in* him. The
gloss's own surrounding argument (*Confitetur Filius apud Patrem illum…*, and the *Ei autem*
that follows about the unforgivable sin) works either way, so the context does not decide it.
Rendered as printed and **not marked**; see §(e).

### 0295D — *Commendanti gratiam supernae pacis et unitatis, vult iste ingerere molestiam*
The dative participle is the Lord who commends; *iste* is the man of Lc 12:13. Bound
accordingly. Mechanism-5 site; checked.

### 0295D — *Inter fratres, patrimonium, non judex medius, sed pietas debet sequestra dividere, quamvis immortalitatis patrimonium non sit pecunia hominibus petendum*
*sequestra* is a feminine noun in apposition to *pietas* ("affection as arbitress"); the final
clause has *patrimonium* (neuter) with *petendum* but *pecunia* (feminine) as predicate, so it
does not agree cleanly. Rendered literally, gender mismatch and all — **Pattern 9**: a Latin
gender mismatch has no English exponent, there is nothing to emend and nothing to mark.

### 0296D — *nec communes fructus aliquo sibi dominatu specialius vindicantibus*
The participle agrees with *volatilibus*, the birds, not with the providence of God. Checked.

### 0297A — *Vestiebat sicut unum*
See §(e). Rendered active, as printed.

### 0297C — *quibus si haec subtrahuntur, ad probationem est, si dantur ad gratiarum actionem*
*quibus* is the faithful of the preceding clause. Note Migne prints no comma before *ad
gratiarum actionem*; not supplied.

### 0298B — the chunk ends mid-argument at *aliis exemplum praebeatis*
Chunk 0020's last column band is 0298B and the sentence closes there. Not a defect; the chunk
division is ours.

---

## (e) DECLINED-ODDITY LIST — every suspect word I did NOT mark

⛔ **This is the list the plate reader works from.** On 8999 the nineteen entries of this list
produced **all seventeen** corrections the work received, while the fired markers produced
none. Each entry says what is odd, names both possible owners, and **asserts neither**.

| # | col | printed | expected | shape / class | why I declined |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **0290B** | ***Aegyto*** | *Aegypto* | **NON-WORD**, one letter (*p*) lost from a proper name | ⚑ *Aegypto* prints **correctly at 0291A**, one column later, in this same chunk. The brief's rule is exact about what that proves: internal corroboration proves an error **exists**, never **whose**. Carried untranslated in italics under Pattern 7, no `[sic:]`. **Plate read owed at 0290B.** |
| 2 | **0293B** | ***Repondens*** *autem* | *Respondens autem* (Lc 11:45) | **NON-WORD**, medial *-s-* lost, inside an italic lemma | Carried inside the lemma with no second italic marker (Pattern 10) and no `[sic:]`. It is a plausible Migne broken sort **and** a plausible transcription drop; I cannot tell. **Plate read owed at 0293B.** |
| 3 | **0293B** | *Qui estis ut monumenta* | *quia estis* (Lc 11:44) | real word, terminal *-a* lost | A one-word divergence, and the shape is what a transcription loses. Drafted as `[var:]`, **withdrawn** under §(a.1). Rendered "Who are as monuments." **Plate read owed at 0293B.** |
| 4 | **0295C** | *verbum **in Filio** hominis* | *in Filium hominis* (Lc 12:10) | real word, one inflection — **but the sense turns on it** | *in Filium* = *against* the Son; *in Filio* = *in* the Son. Rendered as printed. Declined because it is one word, and because a `[var:]` here would assert something about Migne's page I have no evidence for. ⭐ **If one column in this range is read at the plate, make it this one.** |
| 5 | **0297A** | *Vestiebat sicut unum* | *vestiebatur* (Lc 12:27) | real word, active for passive | ⚑ The gloss immediately before reads *operum gloria **vestiebat*** — the exposition is built on the active. That is internal corroboration, which settles nothing about ownership. Drafted as `[var:]`, **withdrawn**. Rendered active. **Plate read owed at 0297A.** |
| 6 | **0288A** | *Quis vestrum **habet** amicum* | *habebit* (Lc 11:5) | real word, *-bi-* lost from a future | Exactly the shape of a transcription slip **and** exactly the shape of a compositor's. Drafted as `[var:]`, **withdrawn**. Rendered "hath". **Plate read owed at 0288A.** |
| 7 | **0284D** | *Vae tibi, **Corozaim*** | *Corozain* (Lc 10:13) | proper name, terminal nasal | Repeated as *Corozaim* in the gloss's own next sentence, so it is at least consistent on our side. Both *-m* and *-n* forms circulate; no marker. Low priority for the plate. |
| 8 | **0287C** | *tanto amplius **cadit*** | *capit* would give the expected sense | real word, one letter | Declined `[cj:]` — argued in full at §(d) 0287C. The printed word construes with the water of the next sentence. **Worth a glance at 0287C.** |
| 9 | **0295A** | *vel **mansuros** in denuntiatione supplicii* | — | real words, **does not construe** | Declined `[cj:]` — argued in full at §(d) 0295A. A future participle in the accusative with no governing word; at least two repairs are available and I will not pick one. **Plate read owed at 0295A.** |
| 10 | **0294B** | *nec ipsi **lege et prophetis** intelligunt* | *in lege et prophetis*? | possible lost preposition | Bare ablative where the idiom wants *in*. A dropped two-letter word is invisible to every test we have. Rendered as printed. |
| 11 | **0294B** | ***Intrare clavem*** | *intrare clave*? | real words, odd case | *intrare* with an accusative *clavem* — "to enter the key". Could be Migne, could be a transcribed *clave*. Rendered literally. |
| 12 | **0289C** | *domus supra domum **cadet** … vel **opprimuntur** vel **puniuntur*** | — | singular verb, then two plurals | A number mismatch across one sentence. Could be Migne's compositor, could be the recension abridging badly. No marker; rendered as printed. |
| 13 | **0291C** | *Qui scilicet tales **spiritus**, nequiores, diabolo dicuntur* | — | real words, broken agreement | *Qui … spiritus* has no clean antecedent and *tales* floats. Rendered literally ("Which spirits, namely such as these, are called more wicked than the devil"). |
| 14 | **0290D** | *(Ephes**,** IV)* | *(Ephes. IV)* | comma for a full stop in an abbreviation | Typographic; inside an italic run, not a `[n:]`. Reproduced verbatim. Named only so a sweep does not "fix" it. |
| 15 | **0295B** | *[n: (I Cor. IX**.**)]* | *(I Cor. IX)* | stray full stop before the closing paren, unlike its eight siblings in this chunk | A `[n:]` passes verbatim. Named only so the difference is on the record. |
| 16 | **0288B** | *[n: (Psal. **XLIV**)]* | Ps CXLIV | citation numeral, an initial *C* absent | Migne's citation, verbatim and uncorrected per the brief and §6.3. Named because the indexer will resolve it to Ps 44, which contains none of the quoted words. |
| 17 | **0290A** | *quod Satanas **Satan** expellat* | — | indeclinable *Satan* beside nominative *Satanas* | Both forms are Vulgate usage; **not** an oddity, listed so a later reader does not re-raise it. |
| 18 | **0292A** | *in regina austri **studium** percipiendae sapientiae* | — | bare nominative in apposition, no verb | The abridgment's compression, most likely. Rendered as an apposition; nothing supplied. |
| 19 | **0285B** | *cum illae civitates praedictae … noluerunt, Tyrus et Sidon olim fuerunt* | — | *cum*-clause with no apodosis | Rendered as printed. The recension cuts its fathers short constantly; this reads like a splice, not a type defect. |

⚑ **Priority for a plate reader, if the range gets fewer leaves than columns:** entries **4**
(0295C, sense-bearing), **1** (0290B, non-word with a clean control one column away), **2**
(0293B, non-word), **9** (0295A, does not construe), then 3, 5, 6.

⚑ **What this list cannot reach.** The brief's second mechanism class — Corpus Corporum
*silently repairing* Migne's broken type — leaves no trace in our file by construction. Nothing
in my range can be offered against it, and a clean-looking Latin here is not evidence of a
clean plate.

# STINT 0021 — chunks 0021–0025, cols 0298B–0310B

# Cruces — 9000, *Glossa ordinaria*, **Evangelium secundum Lucam**, chunks 0021–0025

Stint range: `src/latin/9000/0021.md` … `0025.md` · **cols 0298B–0310C** · 4,988 Latin words
(5,255 by `wc -w` including frontmatter) · CAPUT XII (cont.), XIII, XIII (cont.), XIV, XIV (cont.).

**Apparatus fired in this range: 4 `[var:]` · 0 `[sic:]` · 0 `[cj:]` · 0 `[d:]` · 0 `[ed:]` · 0 `[nt:]`.**
Column anchors reproduced 1:1 (13 · 16 · 4 · 15 · 1 = 49, diffed against the Latin, all in order).
Notes reproduced 1:1 and verbatim (1 · 2 · 0 · 9 · 0 = 12); none is editorial prose, so no `[nt:]` is owed.

---

## (a) THE POLICY THIS STINT APPLIED — stated before the findings, so it can be argued with

1. **No `[sic:]`, none, on any ground.** The brief's measured finding governs: on John, 29,727
   words produced zero fired `[sic:]`, the 23 leaf-reads spent on fired markers produced **no**
   corrections, and the nineteen DECLINED oddities produced **all seventeen**. Every suspect word
   in this range — real word reading oddly, non-word, fused or split run alike — is **rendered as
   printed, logged in §(e), and left unmarked**, with no claim about whose defect it is. I do not
   know whether any of them is Migne's type or Corpus Corporum's transcription, and I say so
   site by site rather than in a preamble only.
2. **No `[cj:]` either.** Pattern 18's bar is that the printed word must make the sentence assert
   something the author is arguing *against*, and 18a's extension reaches negations and
   correlatives only. Four sites in this range are strong conjecture candidates (§e.1, e.4, e.11,
   e.12) and **none** of them is a negation or a correlative; each is a word that leaves the
   sentence obscure rather than false. All four are declined and logged with the conjecture named,
   which is where 18a says an uncertain reading belongs.
3. **`[var:]` is the one marker I fire, and only where a word or clause is REPLACED by different
   matter that changes what the verse says.** Each of the four rests on
   `sources/vulgate/clementine-flat.txt`, cited book chapter:verse, and each sits on a **quoted
   clause** rather than a single word — the class the brief's table shows standing 88 of 88 across
   three Glossa gospels. **Declined, explicitly:** abridgment (a lemma truncated, or closing in
   *etc.*), orthography (*sepes*/*saepes*, *Usquedum*/*usque dum*), preposition variants
   (*super*/*supra*, twice), and every **single-word** substitution — *ei* for *et*, *esse* for
   *est*, an added *se*. A one-word `[var:]` on an unread column is the trap 9003's collation
   exposed, where the digitization *manufactured* the divergence a collation then dutifully found.
4. **Migne's wrong citation ADDRESSES take no marker.** Three notes in this range point at
   chapters that cannot be right (`Rom. XXXV`, `Rom. VIII` for Rom 13:13, `II Cor. IV` for
   1 Cor 4:9). Notes are reproduced verbatim and untranslated; the words Migne quotes agree with
   the Clementine in two of the three, so there is no divergence to mark — only an address to log
   (9001's precedent at 0192B, where a wrong address alone took no `[var:]`).
5. **Pattern 7 literalism, including where it hurts.** *nihil mundamini*, *petierunt*, *domum
   intellectus*, *cognitione conjunctos*, *operatur* stranded at a sentence end, *affectum* for
   *affectuum* — all rendered as the printed words allow, none repaired, none smoothed by a
   substituted English word. Where the printed sentence will not construe cleanly the English is
   correspondingly hard, and the crux says so.
6. **Punctuation follows the plate (Pattern 8).** Migne's `?` closing the fragment *Qua hora non
   putatis Filius hominis veniet?* rides as printed on what is a statement. The paragraph at
   0304A ending *…noxiae inclinationis erigi* has **no terminal stop in the Latin and none was
   supplied in the English.**
7. **§1 conventions carried from Mark and John, from the first line.** *turba* → **crowd**
   (5 sites), *multitudo* → **multitude** (1 site, 0298B, and it is the only one); *daemonium* →
   **demon** (0305C, twice), *diabolus* → **devil** (0301B, twice); *gentes* → **Gentiles** in the
   Judaei/gentes opposition (0304B, 0304A, 0307A, 0309B), singular *gens* of the Jewish people →
   **nation** (0302C *infructuosa gente Judaeorum*, 0306B *tulerunt locum et gentem*); **no
   `natio` occurs anywhere in this range** — checked with word boundaries, because a bare grep for
   `natio` matches *damnatio*, *inclinatio* and *examinatio*, which is the instrument error the
   brief warns about, and it fired here nine times out of eleven. *mysterium* → mystery,
   *sacramentum* → sacrament, 1:1. **`usque ad` → "as far as"**, every occurrence: two splice
   formulas (0299C in the AMBR. extract, 0308D in the AUG. extract) and two ordinary uses
   (0300A *usque ad finem saeculi*, 0300B *usque ad perfectionem*).
8. **The gloss's own voice is not archaised.** *dicit*/*ait* → **says/said**, no `saith`. Every
   `-eth` form in the range was audited: all of them sit inside an italic scripture lemma
   (*cometh*, *knocketh*, *possesseth*). One `-eth` in the gloss voice was written and then
   removed — *"it behoveth thee"* at 0300C is now *"thou oughtest"*, which keeps Pattern 17's
   required `-est` on a singular address without archaising a third-person gloss verb. The `-est`
   forms that remain (*goest*, *wouldest*, *mayest*, *purposest*) are all Pattern 17 singulars.
9. **Pattern 17 in the second person.** The gloss switches number inside single paragraphs and the
   English follows the morphology, not the addressee: *thou* at 0300C (*posthabeas*), 0301B
   (*vadis, praeparas, tuae carni*), 0307A–0308A (*invitatus fueris, te junxeris, extollas,
   incipias, distribueris*); **you** at 0301C (*vivite … damnemini*) and 0309B (*Voca pauperes*
   context, plural imperatives).
10. **The Greek is reproduced in Greek.** `Ὕδωρ` stands at the head of 0024 exactly as printed,
    space before the comma included, not transliterated and not translated away (§d, 0306C).

## (b) SPAN COUNT AGAINST THE BRIEF — 142 vs 142, COUNTED

`data/briefs/9000-lemmata-0021.txt` declares **142 spans** for chunks 0021–0025.
I counted the `*…*` runs in the body of my own five Latin chunks (frontmatter excluded):

| chunk | italic spans |
|---|---|
| 0021 | 37 |
| 0022 | 37 |
| 0023 | 14 |
| 0024 | 51 |
| 0025 | 3 |
| **total** | **142** |

**142 = 142. The brief's split is correct for this range**, and every line of it is walked below,
in the brief's own order, agreements included — because a report of findings alone cannot be told
apart from a report by an agent that never looked.

## (c) THE LEMMA DIVERGENCE LIST — all 142 spans, with the reason for every decline

### Chunk 0021 — spans 1–37 (bands 0298B–0301C)

1. [0298B] VERS. 36.-- *Exspectantibus.*   — single word, check in place
   → AGREES Lc 12:36.

2. [0298B] *Quando revertatur.*   ✓ Clementine verbatim
   → AGREES Lc 12:36.

3. [0298B] *Ut cum venerit, confestim aperiant ei.*   ⚠ NOT in Clementine verbatim — CHECK
   → **DIVERGES — `[var:]` FIRED.** Vulg Lc 12:36 *ut, cum venerit et pulsaverit, confestim aperiant ei*. Migne's lemma drops *et pulsaverit* from the middle of the run, and the gloss immediately after it expounds precisely the knocking (*Non vult aperire judici pulsanti*). An internal omission, not a truncation, and the exposition rests on the words that are gone: fired, cited Lc 12:36.

   → AGREES Lc 12:37. Cue truncated, closing in *etc.*

   → **DIVERGES, one word — DECLINED.** Vulg Lc 12:41 *Ait autem et Petrus*; Migne prints *ei*. A one-word divergence on an unread column is the class 9001's cruces names as the trap the manufactured *judicabit*/*judicavit* set: it is as likely to be our transcription as his type, and `[var:]` would make it a public claim about the plate. Logged, not marked; the English reads "And Peter said to him."

   → **DIVERGES, one word — DECLINED.** Vulg Lc 12:42 *Quis, putas, est fidelis dispensator*; Migne prints *esse*, and the English renders the infinitive it is ("Who dost thou think to be the faithful steward?"). The span also carries the head of 12:49 before it: that is the recension's own splice of the two verses it is about to expound together, not a divergence in either.
